## Switzerland added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Switzerland's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **8 files / 6 works, and no pupil textbook among them**: the three PER brochures *Mathématiques et Sciences de la nature / Sciences humaines et sociales*, Cycles 1, 2 and 3 (CIIP, Version 3.0, août 2024), whose Mathématiques parts are placed here; the Ticino *Piano di studio della scuola dell'obbligo ticinese* (DECS, edizione perfezionata 2022), whose Matematica chapter is placed here; the *Lehrplan 21 Graubünden* (Fassung vom 15.03.2016), whose Mathematik plan is placed here — one work in three filed language editions (German, Romansh, Italian), read in full in the German edition, verified identical in structure and pagination in the other two, and cited once; and one teacher guide, *MaMa — Linee guida del progetto MaMa: Ambito Numeri e calcolo* (DECS/CeRDD, 2021), whose didactic rulings are placed here as teacher evidence — 425 items in all (364 curriculum evidence + 61 teacher-guide evidence, 0 pupil). The expansion map's kind split is WRONG (record-level; the records themselves are not edited — merges never edit the records): it recorded "3 student + 1 teacher", but the three "student" files are the PER **curriculum** brochures mislabelled — **Switzerland has 0 pupil Maths textbooks in hand**, and its prescribed pupil series (Schweizer Zahlenbuch, Mathematik Primarstufe / Sekundarstufe I (LMVZ), Mathwelt, Mathbuch, MER Mathématiques 1–11, Ticino's Base Matematica 1–4) are commercial and licence-gated, not in hand and not read. Further maths was found hiding under "all subjects" labels: the Ticino Piano and the Lehrplan 21 Graubünden are all-subjects volumes carrying full mathematics chapters. Every grabbed file was verified SHA-256 byte-exact against its books.json record. Checked in hand and excluded with reasons, stocked nowhere: the three PER *Aperçus des contenus* (one example-grain maths page each — record items R1–R3), the PER *Présentation générale* (framework structure only), the PER *Éducation numérique* brochure (the computer-science curriculum; mathematics appears only as cross-links — a separate school subject) and the four Vaud *<DÉ>CODAGE* digital-education teacher manuals (contents pages of all four read: Science informatique / Médias / Usages only — a separate school subject); inside the PER brochures, the Sciences de la nature and Sciences humaines et sociales parts are other subjects, the *Modélisation* objectives are process apparatus whose content is carried by the per-theme problem-solving items, and the Lexiques are apparatus; inside the Ticino volume, every chapter other than Matematica is another subject and the process/attitude competence targets carry no subject content; inside the Lehrplan 21 volumes, every subject other than Mathematik is out of scope and the Einführung/Gesamtüberblick chapters are structure, not content; MaMa §§1 and 3 are project apparatus, and §2's quoted Ticino targets are placed at the Ticino items, not double-counted (record item R4). One within-document inconsistency is flagged so it is not stocked: the PER Cycle 1 *Aperçus des contenus* claims Cycle 1 pupils "calculate lengths (perimeter of a rectangle), areas, volumes (cube)" — the governing Cycle 1 brochure requires only comparison and measurement with an étalon (items 46–55), and perimeter/area/volume calculation begins in Cycle 2 (items 158–160); the brochure governs, the Aperçu's Cycle 1 sentence is an overstatement, and it is NOT stocked as a Cycle 1 requirement (record item R1). One document error is called out by a source itself and taught corrected: the MaMa guide rules that there is no "dissociative property" of addition — splitting an addend and associating (16 + 19 = 16 + 10 + 9) is the associative property, and textbooks that name it otherwise are wrong (item 378); the guide's other flagged classroom errors are misconceptions it warns against, not errors it commits. No Liechtenstein file is judged here: Liechtenstein teaches from Switzerland's books, but no file is filed under Liechtenstein in any record. Printed-page basis: PER brochures, Ticino Piano and Lehrplan 21 — printed folio = PDF page − 2; MaMa — printed page = PDF page. Switzerland's class for Maths is **PARTIAL (evidence-only)**: every in-hand Maths-bearing file has been read and stocked, but all 425 of its items are curriculum or teacher-guide evidence and no pupil textbook is in hand. Into this lesson the placements stock 11 items (7 curriculum evidence and 4 teacher evidence) from Book 1, Book 2 and Book 5. Switzerland is added to the countries line and named in the rows below as evidence only, and is NOT added to the acceptance count, on the standing evidence-only precedent.

**Book 1:** *Mathématiques et Sciences de la nature / Sciences humaines et sociales — Cycle 1* (curriculum brochure) — Switzerland, CIIP (Conférence intercantonale de l'instruction publique de la Suisse romande et du Tessin), Plan d'études romand (PER), Version 3.0, août 2024; 899,121 bytes, 76 PDF pp.; SHA-256 verified exact against its books.json record. Printed folio = PDF p. − 2. Only the Mathématiques part (printed pp. 13–21) is placed here; the Sciences de la nature and Sciences humaines et sociales parts of the same brochure are other subjects. Filed in the records as a student book — wrong: it is a curriculum brochure. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — the brochure states what must be taught and teaches nothing itself; its rows are worded as the requirements they are and are counted in no lesson's taught-in figure.

**Book 2:** *Mathématiques et Sciences de la nature / Sciences humaines et sociales — Cycle 2* (curriculum brochure) — Switzerland, CIIP (Conférence intercantonale de l'instruction publique de la Suisse romande et du Tessin), Plan d'études romand (PER), Version 3.0, août 2024; 1,209,759 bytes, 116 PDF pp.; SHA-256 verified exact against its books.json record. Printed folio = PDF p. − 2. Only the Mathématiques part (printed pp. 14–29) is placed here; the Sciences de la nature and Sciences humaines et sociales parts of the same brochure are other subjects. Filed in the records as a student book — wrong: it is a curriculum brochure. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — the brochure states what must be taught and teaches nothing itself; its rows are worded as the requirements they are and are counted in no lesson's taught-in figure.

**Book 5:** *MaMa — Linee guida del progetto MaMa: Ambito Numeri e calcolo* (teacher guide) — Switzerland, © MaMa – DECS – Divisione della scuola (CeRDD), 2021; 16,669,707 bytes, 102 PDF pp.; SHA-256 verified exact against its books.json record. Printed p. = PDF p. Read in full, cover to bibliography; §§1 and 3 (project history, platform, community) are apparatus, stocked nowhere, and §2's quoted Ticino Piano targets are placed at the Ticino items, not double-counted (record item R4). TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — the guide directs teachers how to teach numbers and calculation; it is not a pupil textbook, and its rows are counted in no lesson's taught-in figure.

Switzerland enters this lesson as evidence only: it is named in the countries line and in the rows above, but it is NOT added to the acceptance count — Switzerland has no pupil Maths textbook in hand; its in-hand set is three curriculum brochures, the mathematics chapters of two all-subjects curriculum volumes and one teacher guide, which state requirements and teaching guidance without being pupil textbooks (the standing evidence-only precedent). Switzerland's class for Maths is PARTIAL (evidence-only): the whole in-hand set is read and stocked, and it advances only when a Swiss pupil Maths textbook — one of the prescribed commercial series, today licence-gated — lands in hand and is read.

## Timor-Leste added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Timor-Leste's whole in-hand library from the PDFs — **5 files**, of which **2 carry mathematics**: the Grade 5 pupil exercise book *Livru Ezersísiu Matemátika 5* and the Matemátika section (printed pp. 318–511) of *Livru Planu Lisaun Klase 3 Períodu 2*, the combined all-subject Grade 3 Period-2 lesson-plan volume. The other three files were checked in full and carry no maths teaching: *Livru Dalan Tetun ba Klase 5* (a Tetum grammar book — number words appear once, as one of eight grammatical word-classes), *Livru Planu Lisaun Klase 6 Períodu 2* (Natural and Social Science plans only; its four “matemátika” mentions sit inside one density lesson) and *Um Mundo de Histórias 6º ano* (a Portuguese reader; maths appears only inside stories). The expansion map's “1 student + 0 teacher” split counts only the books filed as Maths and repeats a filing error — **flag F1** (record-side: reported here, not fixed; merges never edit the records, and no `maths/books/timor-leste.md` filing exists): the Grade 3 volume is filed as the “Ciências Naturais Grade 3 Teacher Guide”, claiming Natural Science plans from p. 10 and Social Science plans from p. 468, but its own Índise (printed p. 3) lists all nine basic-education components and puts Matemátika at p. 318, Siénsia Naturál at p. 512 and Siénsia Sosiál at p. 624 — the mis-filing hid the country's only in-hand Maths teacher evidence. **Flag F2** (record gap): `textbooks.tsv` carries only 3 Timor-Leste rows — the two Grade 5 books, including the Maths exercise book, sit in `books.json`, the push state and the GitHub repo tree with no TSV row. Everything else Timor-Leste Maths is link-only and not in hand — the Matemática G1–G4 student books, the G10–G12 student manuals, the 2024 new-curriculum Grade 7 set and the Learning Passport book courses — and is cited nowhere. The placements number **177 items: 83 pupil-workbook items and 94 teacher-evidence items** (33 from full printed lesson plans; 61 from the period plan table alone, each marked “(plan table only)”). The workbook is a genuine pupil teaching book — 77 numbered exercise sets (Ezersísiu 1–77) plus two front-matter reading tables — but it practises and tests; it does not present topics first (its letter to teachers states the exercises follow the annual plan and lesson-plan sequence), and it is not the main Grade 5 textbook. The guide's Matemátika section is **Period 2 only** — the Period 1 and Period 3 volumes are not in hand, so Grade 3 coverage here is one period of three. The books' own errors are taught corrected: **E1** — the workbook's census table heads its second column “2005”, but its figures are the 2010 census and its questions ask about 2010; **E2** — Ezersísiu 70 is a reprint of Ezersísiu 68, counted once (as item 70); **E3** — Ezersísiu 60's fourth prism has equal dimensions in the Tetum text and different dimensions in the Portuguese (the Tetum governs); **E4** — the guide's Lisaun 51 plan tells the teacher to write “6 x 1 = 1” (read 6 x 1 = 6, as the page's own explanation gives); **E5** — the guide's introduction states 2 formal formative assessments per period while the plan schedules 6 (assessments are exclusions either way); **E6** — ten blank plan-table content cells are continuation/test rows, folded into their parent items, and lesson 84's body settles it as the fraction–decimal relation lesson. The placements' named exclusions are stocked nowhere: the workbook's covers, imprint, forewords, letters and “do not write in this book” notice; the guide's discipline introduction and assessment guidance (printed pp. 326–328); its 22 consolidation lessons, 6 formal-assessment lessons and 5 project lessons (no new content stated for any of them); blank continuation/test rows; homework and in-lesson revision steps; and the Grade 3 volume's other eight subject sections. Counting grain: pupil book — one item per distinct practised skill; teacher guide — one item per distinct taught content in the period plan. Timor-Leste's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — a pupil-workbook set on the Peru pattern: the workbook's rows count, the guide's rows are teacher evidence and count nowhere. Into this lesson the placements stock 12 items: 2 pupil-workbook + 10 teacher evidence.

**Book 1:** *Livru Ezersísiu Matemátika 5* (pupil workbook — the Grade 5 Mathematics exercise book) — Ministério da Educação de Timor-Leste; bilingual Tetum/Portuguese; based on the 2014 National Basic Education Curriculum (Cycles 1–2), 1st edition May 2016, print run 38,900; 1,997,219 bytes, 70 imposed sheets (140 half-pages), MD5 44cd93b05692baaa17cc58569dc9cc5b. Complete as filed: Ezersísiu 1–77 all present between the front cover/imprint/forewords block and the back cover. **No folio is printed anywhere in the book** — the PDF is a printer's imposition, two portrait book pages per landscape sheet — so its items are cited by exercise number (Ezersísiu N). PUPIL WORKBOOK — its rows count in a lesson's taught-in figure (the Peru workbook pattern).

**Book 2:** *Livru Planu Lisaun Klase 3 Períodu 2* (teacher guide — the combined all-subject Grade 3 Period-2 lesson-plan volume; its Matemátika section only) — Ministério da Educação de Timor-Leste; 15,881,272 bytes, 829 PDF pp., MD5 18b0a4e0d55af5f56868c556dc9893f2. Its Índise (printed p. 3) lists all nine basic-education components; the Matemátika section (printed pp. 318–511) was read in full — full 50-minute lesson plans (objective, materials, timed teaching steps; the MAT3.x codes are the national curriculum's Grade 3 outcomes) plus the period plan table at printed pp. 318–325. Printed page = PDF page. TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY — its rows are named in a lesson but counted in no taught-in figure, and the section is Period 2 only. Filed in the library records as the “Ciências Naturais Grade 3 Teacher Guide” — the filing error F1 carried in the intro above.

## Uganda added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Uganda's whole in-hand Maths library from the PDFs — **8 files / 8 works: 7 pupil books + 1 teacher's guide**, all Uganda Ministry of Education and Sports / NCDC *Home-Study Learning* books (August 2020) — and the expansion map's “7 student + 1 teacher, FILES-IN-HAND” count and split are **correct**, with one correction of kind: the Primary Five pupil book is not a standalone Maths book — it is the Mathematics booklet (package PDF pp. 9–166) inside the NCDC Primary Five merged home-study package (Mathematics + Integrated Science + English + Social Studies, 350 pp.). Maths content hiding under an all-subjects label was checked for across the whole in-hand set; this package is the only such volume. The seven pupil books (Primary Four, the Primary Five booklet, Primary Six, Senior One, Senior Two, Senior Three, Senior Four) are real pupil teaching books — lessons, worked examples and exercises addressed to the learner — so Uganda's pupil rows **count** in a lesson's taught-in figure. The one teacher book, the Senior One *Teacher's Guide* (prototype, 76 pp.), is **teacher evidence only** and counts nowhere; it covers all 12 Senior One prototype topics, including five topics (1 Number Bases, 2 Working with Integers, 8 Angle Properties, 9 Data Collection and Presentation, 12 Time and Time Tables) for which no Senior One pupil text is in hand, and its evidence for those topics carries that “no pupil book in hand” caveat. NOT in hand, and cited nowhere: the Senior One prototype pupil Mathematics textbook (NCDC posted only the Teacher's Guide) and the Scribd combined S1&S2 Science Package (no download route). The placements number **324 items: 278 pupil items and 46 teacher-evidence items (TG1–TG46)** — one item per distinct lesson in the home-study books, one per distinct topic treatment in the guide. Page citations are PDF pages for the pupil books (in the primary home-study books the printed folios run 6 behind: printed = PDF − 6; Primary Five pages are the merged package's PDF pages) and the guide's printed pages for the TG items. The books' own errors are taught corrected: the Senior One home-study book heads the conversion example at PDF p. 8 “Convert 13/4 into an improper fraction” — 13/4 is already improper, and the worked example in fact converts it to the mixed number 3¼; the Primary Six book's triangle-angles exercise (PDF p. 85) prints two interior angles as “360 and 540” — the degree signs set as zeros, read as 36° and 54°; the Senior Three book heads its mensuration topic “TRIGONOMETRY” while its own stated outcomes and lessons are units, surface area and volume — taught as mensuration. Uganda's own ways are kept: equations written with a shape for the unknown before letters (Primary Four); integers walked on a ground number line, face forward for positive and back for backward (Primary Five and Six); base five taught in primary school (Primary Five); fractions taught by slicing chapattis (Senior One); income tax worked from Uganda's own tax bands and allowances (Senior Three). Uganda's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** — every Maths book in hand has been read fresh and placed; what remains outside the set (the Senior One prototype pupil textbook, the Senior Two–Four prototype books and guides) is not in hand and is a library hunting matter, not a course hold. Into this lesson the placements stock 3 pupil items.

**Book 1:** *Home-Study Learning, Primary Five* — the Mathematics booklet only (pupil book) — Uganda, Ministry of Education and Sports / NCDC, August 2020; the Primary Five book is not a standalone Maths book: it is the Mathematics booklet (package PDF pp. 9–166, 158 pp.) inside the merged home-study package (Mathematics + Integrated Science + English + Social Studies); the package file is 10,444,968 bytes, 350 PDF pp., MD5 5159920c66cfd1869ff19fd9cf119fea, and the booklet's interior is verified as the Primary Five Mathematics home-study book. Pages are cited by the package's PDF page. PUPIL BOOK — its rows count in a lesson's taught-in figure.

**Book 2:** *Mathematics Home-Study Learning, Primary Six* (pupil book) — Uganda, Ministry of Education and Sports / NCDC, August 2020; 1,758,562 bytes, 98 PDF pp., MD5 2046f27f8e3df0cc1c9b274f5b17a776. Cover and NCDC colophon read directly. Pages are cited by PDF page; the printed folios run 6 behind (printed = PDF − 6). PUPIL BOOK — its rows count in a lesson's taught-in figure.

## Samoa added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Samoa's whole in-hand Maths library from the PDFs — **9 files: 8 pupil textbooks + 1 curriculum statement, 0 teacher guides** — and the expansion map's “7 student Maths books, 0 teacher” count is **wrong**: the map missed the *Mathematics Year 12* typeset edition, staged in the records as a supposed workbook (“full-year-wk”) but proved from its title page and interior to be the complete MESC Year 12 pupil textbook (Units 1–8 + answers), and it missed the kind of the *Primary Curriculum Statement, Years 1–8*, which is a curriculum document, not a pupil book. The library's enumerated slot for the separate 113 MB scanned Year 12 edition is NOT in hand (live 404; only a truncated 1 MB archive stub exists) — that edition is cited nowhere and the slot stays a library-side matter; the complete typeset Year 12 pupil textbook in hand is the book read. No teacher guide for Maths exists in the Samoa set, and no Maths content was found hiding under another subject label. The eight pupil books are real pupil teaching books — lessons, worked examples and exercises addressed to the learner — so Samoa's pupil rows **count** in a lesson's taught-in figure. The curriculum statement (CMAD, MESC, January 2013) states what is REQUIRED to be taught in Samoa primary schools and teaches nothing itself: its rows are **curriculum evidence only** and count in no taught-in figure — Samoa has no primary pupil textbooks in hand, so every primary (Years 1–8) Samoa row in the course is curriculum evidence. Counting grain: one item per distinct teaching section of a pupil book (the book's own numbered sections and their named subsections), and one item per coded learning outcome (NR / PA / DA / MS / SG / WM codes) of the curriculum statement; worked examples and exercise sets inside a section are part of that section's item, not separate items. The books' own errors are taught corrected: Year 10 Book 2's §7.1 heading prints “Drawing an Englargement” (for Enlargement); Year 11 Book 2's unit opener prints “Dealing with Angles Larger than 900” (for 90°); Year 13's contents lists chapter 2 as “Linear Inequations” while the chapter's printed title is “Linear Equations” and it teaches both — placed under the printed title. Named exclusions, stocked nowhere: the answers sections of every pupil book; Year 13's “Problems and Investigations” end-of-chapter sections, its Glossary/Index and its formula Appendix; calculator-keystroke asides (the mathematics is placed, the keystrokes are not); the curriculum statement's strand progression tables (printed pp. 81–120), which restate its coded outcomes; and Year 12's Unit 8 project unit, placed as one item. Into this lesson the placements stock 7 items: 7 curriculum evidence.

**Book 1:** *Mathematics — Primary Curriculum Statement, Years 1–8* (curriculum statement) — CMAD, Ministry of Education, Sports and Culture (MESC), January 2013; ISBN 978-982-517-089-1; written by Prof. Karoline Afamasaga-Fuata'i; 1,601,230 bytes, 120 PDF pp., MD5 ebb1213da1d69215fd1724aaf018ed34. Title page read directly. Printed page = PDF page. CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY — the statement states what is required to be taught in Samoa primary schools and teaches nothing itself; its rows are named in a lesson but counted in no taught-in figure. ## Turkmenistan added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Turkmenistan's whole filed in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **5 pupil files = 3 pupil works, plus 1 teacher guide** (6 documents, 840 PDF pages in all) — and the expansion map's "3 student + 0 teacher" kind split is **wrong** (**flag F1**: the map counts one file per grade/track slot; it misses the second parts of both Turkmen works and the teacher guide entirely — any plan sized on "3 books" under-sizes the read by about 2.5×). The pupil works are the complete Turkmen-medium Grade 1 and Grade 2 courses — *Matematika* Parts 1 and 2 for each grade (Atabaýew & Öwezowa, 3rd editions, 2019/2020) — and Part 1 only of the Russian-medium Grade 1 course (Rulieva, Chernikova, Mavlyanova & Satdykova, 4th ed. 2022), a separate work by a separate author team, not a translation of the Turkmen pair; the two Grade 1 Turkmen books are scans with no text layer and were read from page renders. The one teacher book, N. Açilowa's *Başlangyç synplarda matematikany okatmagyň usulyýeti* (Methodology of Teaching Mathematics in the Primary Classes, Ylym, 2015), is a teacher-training study guide: it teaches NO pupil content, so its rows are **teacher evidence only** and count in no lesson's taught-in figure; the five pupil books are real pupil textbooks, recommended by the Ministry of Education of Turkmenistan and served by the Ministry's own E-kitaphana, so their rows **count**. **Flag F2** (record-side: reported here, not fixed; merges never edit the records, and no `maths/books/turkmenistan.md` filing exists): a `books.json` note claims the Russian Grade 1 Part 2 "is E-kitaphana post 507" — post 507 is in fact **Grade 2 Russian Mathematics Part 1** (per the enumeration table and the grid match), no Grade 1 Russian Part 2 post exists in the catalogue, and that Part 2 is recorded nowhere as found; post 507 must not be double-counted as both a Grade 1 and a Grade 2 book, and the Russian Grade 1 work stands in hand as Part 1 only. **Flag F3**: the filing notes for these books rested on samples ("every-fifth-page verification", contents lists not yet verified); this full re-read confirms the sampled claims spot-checked — e.g. the kilogram section of Grade 1 (Turkmen) Part 2 does sit at printed pp. 63–64 — and supplies the missing contents lists for both Grade 2 parts (Part 1 contents at PDF p. 103; Part 2 at PDF pp. 118–119); no sampled content claim checked was found false. **Flag F4**: the map's "2 link-only" figure cannot be confirmed and looks far too low — the banked E-kitaphana grid match carries Maths links for the Grade 2 Russian pair and for Grades 3–9 Mathematics, Algebra and Geometry, plus a Grade 5 methodology guide; every one of those is link-only, not in hand and cited nowhere, and everything beyond Grade 2 is outside this set. Byte-identical duplicate posts are cited once, on the filed copy (post 403 = the Grade 1 Part 2 file; post 412 = the Grade 2 Part 1 file; post 418 = the Grade 2 Part 2 file; post 576 = the teacher guide's post 555 file), and every pupil file was pulled from its GitHub release asset at the exact recorded byte size (reconcile sweep #8 lists 23/23 Turkmenistan entries secured). Counting grain: one item per distinct teaching section of a book (its contents-list section, or a headed teaching block where the contents list runs broader), with consolidation/revision sections counted once per book-part; pure exercise pages inside a section are apparatus of that section's item. In every book in this set the printed folio equals the PDF page. One reading note, carried exactly: in the Grade 1 (Turkmen) Part 1 book's type, the empty answer box prints as a small square — in the PDF font layer it can appear as «*» (e.g. the comparing exercises "7 * 6" mean "put <, > or = between 7 and 6") — it is NOT a multiplication sign, and multiplication is not taught in that book. Not stocked as separate rows, exactly as the placements direct: the Grade 2 Part 1 money item 14 (manat notes and coins inside addition/subtraction stories at printed pp. 50, 61 — context only, no money section is taught in that book, and the placements count it inside items 11–13), and the four consolidation/revision items the placements assign to no lesson (Grade 2 Part 1 item 17, Grade 2 Part 2 item 42, Grade 1 Part 1 item 71, Grade 1 Russian Part 1 item 125) — practice over sections already stocked, no new content. Turkmenistan's class for Maths is **COURSES-CLOSED** on the as-filed set. Into this lesson the placements stock 9 items: 8 pupil + 1 teacher evidence.

**Book 1:** *Matematika (II kitap)* — Grade 2, Part 2 (Turkmen-medium pupil textbook) — M. Atabaýew & Z. Öwezowa, 3rd edition, Aşgabat: Türkmen döwlet neşirýat gullugy, 2020; recommended by the Ministry of Education of Turkmenistan; Ministry E-kitaphana post 528 (the byte-identical post 418 is the same file, cited once); 10,667,169 bytes, 120 PDF pp.; grabbed from its GitHub release asset (mid-size-books-8) at the exact recorded byte size. Printed page = PDF page. PUPIL TEXTBOOK — its rows count in a lesson's taught-in figure.

**Book 2:** *Başlangyç synplarda matematikany okatmagyň usulyýeti* [Methodology of Teaching Mathematics in the Primary Classes] (teacher-training study guide) — N. Açilowa, Aşgabat: Ylym, 2015; recommended by the Ministry of Education of Turkmenistan; a study-methodology guide for higher and secondary pedagogical schools; Ministry E-kitaphana post 555 (post 576 is the same file, cited once); 4,173,475 bytes, 272 PDF pp.; read from the copy held in the E-kitaphana work folder at the exact recorded byte size. The staging PDF was deleted after pushing (the pusher's normal pattern); the read copy was grabbed from the recorded source via its banked Wayback capture (2019-11-17 `id_` replay of education.gov.vu/docs/textbooks/Matematiks_2017.pdf) and is byte-identical to the record: 11,818,504 bytes, 317 PDF pages, SHA-256 2b7aa33d2ee0a81d9512e4f141c208bfc04a0abdf6d5bf77d58356a31cf22ec6, matching the staging `books.json` hash exactly. Printed folio = PDF page \u2212 1 throughout (verified on the outcomes section, weekly lesson footers and the glossary). Counting grain: one item per distinct teaching topic in the Year 3 column of the guide's Learning Outcomes & Indicators tables (printed pp. 12\u201322), worded from the outcome and its indicators and confirmed against the weekly lessons that teach it (Terms 1\u20133, printed pp. 23\u2013294); items taught in a weekly lesson but stated in no Year 3 outcome are marked (D). Assessment questions, resources lists and vocabulary boxes are apparatus for the counted items, not separate items. The placements' named exclusions are stocked nowhere: the introduction apart from R3 (printed pp. 4\u201310 — lesson planning, literacy and inclusive-education guidance, teaching craft only); the Beginning/Year 1/Year 2 progression columns; the weekly vocabulary boxes, resources lists and assessment apparatus; and syllabus/timetable exposition carried as R3 only. Book errors E1\u2013E5 are carried taught corrected where they affect stocking: E1 — printed p. 15 abbreviates millilitres "(mm)" (mm is millimetres; millilitres are mL) and writes litres "(l)"; E2 — the Term 3 Week 3 length-conversion lesson (printed p. 233) is headed with the operations outcome NO.3.V1 though it is measurement; E3 — the money lesson (printed p. 202) cites its outcome as "M3.V1" for MM.3.V1; E4 — the Year 3 probability outcome code is printed "CPD.3.V1" (printed p. 21) against the CDP codes of the other columns; E5 — template slips in the term overviews (printed pp. 23, 210), none affecting content. ## Trinidad and Tobago added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Trinidad and Tobago's whole in-hand Maths-bearing set from the files — **9 files, all curriculum/teacher evidence, 0 pupil textbooks**: the dedicated *Primary School Curriculum — Curriculum Guides: Mathematics (Infant 1 – Standard 5)* (MoE, Curriculum Planning and Development Division, 2013), read in full; seven per-level all-subject Primary Curriculum Guides (Infants 1, Infants 2, Standards 1–5), recorded in every record under the plain “Curriculum Guide” label, whose Mathematics sections are the **same curriculum text** as the dedicated guide's section for that level — verified file by file (normalised-text sequence similarity 0.9973 Infants 1, 0.9977 Infants 2, 0.9975 Standard 2, 0.9986 Standard 5; word-multiset containment 1.0000 Standard 1 and 0.9948 Standard 4, whose per-level productions extract in a different column order; Standard 3's scanned guide verified by OCR — rationale “What Is Mathematics About?” at PDF p. 60, “MATHEMATICS: STANDARD 3” tables at PDF pp. 70/80/90, three content spot-checks matching) — so they are **cited once, through the dedicated guide** (placements Record items R1–R7), never double-counted; and the *National Early Childhood Care and Education Curriculum Guide* (ages 3–4), whose mathematics strand is placed separately (items 245–249) because the dedicated guide starts at Infant 1. The expansion map's set picture (0 student + 1 teacher) is wrong as a set description: the dedicated guide it counted is real, but the true in-hand set is these 9 files, and **no pupil Maths textbook for Trinidad and Tobago exists in hand under any label** (all pupil textbooks are commercial — STP Caribbean Mathematics and others; none is held). Every document in the set is a Ministry curriculum guide — a document that states what is REQUIRED to be taught; it teaches nothing itself — so every Trinidad and Tobago item is a curriculum REQUIREMENT item, worded as curriculum evidence. Counting grain: one item per numbered Outcome in the guide's OUTCOMES column, with the placements' stated folds (a bare “solve problems involving X” outcome that restates its own section is folded into that section's item; per-measure vocabulary outcomes are folded into their measure's item; each strand's closing Language outcome is its own item). The placements' named exclusions are stocked nowhere: the dedicated guide's front matter (PDF pp. 1–26 — contents, National Curriculum Framework prose, Subject Rationale); the Dispositions columns of every table (attitudes and values — apparatus, never items); the per-level guides' non-Mathematics subjects; the 17 Forms 1–3 guides staged at grade-08 (ELA, ICT, Spanish, Integrated Science, Social Sciences, VAPA, Visual Arts, Dance, Drama, Music, PE, HFLE, Technology Education — checked book by book; none is a Mathematics book; the Social Sciences staging anchor was labelled “Teacher's Guide” in its source bundle, but the file itself is the Social Sciences Curriculum Guide — a records mislabel, noted, not a Maths item); and the ECCE guide's non-mathematics strands and apparatus-level mentions (§5.9.9 equipment list; §5.9.16 a number line 1–10 in a materials list). Book errors are read corrected: in Standard 4's Number Patterns table the Outcomes-column wording of Outcome 5 is displaced across Outcomes 4 and 6 in print — the requirement was read from its elaborations (the items 183/184 boundary) and is placed corrected; a Standard 4 Decimals conversion elaboration cross-cites code [1.1.5] where [1.1.15] is meant, read as 1.1.15; and fraction glyphs (halves, quarters, fifths, tenths) are dropped at several points in the dedicated guide's text layer — the fractions were read from context and the surrounding elaborations. Set boundary: the *NCSE Mathematics Curriculum Guide (Forms 1–3)* is **link-only, not in hand** — exact links known (official MoE blob copy 2,900,122 B; IBE/UNESCO mirror 2,587,043 B) but the file was never staged or downloaded, so it was not read and is cited nowhere; the NCSE Mathematics *Teacher's* Guide has no public PDF on any route checked by the library fronts (provisional negative). Trinidad and Tobago is **CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY** in every lesson: every item is worded as what the guide requires, Trinidad and Tobago is named in the countries line and in the rows below, and it is added to NO lesson's acceptance count, on the Barbados/Ghana precedent. Trinidad and Tobago's class for Maths is **PARTIAL (evidence-only)**. Into this lesson the placements stock 6 curriculum-evidence item(s) from Book 1.

Curriculum guide — CURRICULUM EVIDENCE ONLY: it states what is required to be taught and teaches nothing itself. Printed page = PDF page − 3 throughout. Read in full (items 1–244: Infant 1 printed pp. 24–44; Infant 2 printed pp. 45–72; Standard 1 printed pp. 73–115; Standard 2 printed pp. 116–161; Standard 3 printed pp. 162–199; Standard 4 printed pp. 200–239; Standard 5 printed pp. 240–261).

## Zimbabwe added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Zimbabwe's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **9 files, all teacher's guides, 0 pupil books**: the Secondary Book Press *PlusOne* (Heritage-Based Curriculum) Mathematics Teacher's Guides for ECD A, ECD B and Grades 1–7. The expansion map is CORRECT for Zimbabwe Maths (0 student + 9 teacher) — no correction. Every file was grabbed from GitHub via the authenticated API (the publisher source URLs returned a Cloudflare challenge page, not the PDFs — a route failure, recorded as placements note N2 and not used) and matches its staging `books.json` record on byte size, SHA-256 and pdfinfo page count exactly (745 PDF pages in total); title/imprint pages were read directly, and contents-against-pages completeness was checked for every book — all 9 are COMPLETE full guides, no sampler or extract among them. No Maths content hides under another subject label: the other 42 in-hand Zimbabwe files are the same PlusOne TG series in English, ChiShona, Science and Technology, Social Science and Physical Education and Arts — none is an "all subjects" volume and none carries Maths units. The ZIMSAKE learner's books once counted were proved publisher SAMPLERS and removed in 2026-11 — 8 of 8 ZIMSAKE files ever pulled are proven samplers, none is a Maths book and none is in hand; the Grade 7 Mathematics *Revision Guide* is not in hand and is an excluded class in any case (revision guides do not count). Counting grain: one item per contents sub-lesson (ECD guides, whose units are sequences of workbook-page lessons) or per numbered unit (Grades 1–7), split only where one unit plainly teaches two distinct course lessons. Pages cited are the books' printed pages (printed folio = PDF page − 4 in the ECD guides; Grades 1–7 unit starts are the contents printed pages, verified against the folios). Book errors found on the read are taught corrected and recorded: the Grade 4 contents lists Unit 17 Money at printed p. 59 and Unit 18 Time at p. 58 — the page order in the contents is reversed; the units themselves run Money (objectives printed p. 56) before Time, and the Money placement cites the objectives page; the Grade 6 Unit 23 (Time) guide cites its Learner's Book as "pages 18−204" — a dropped digit (the unit's own exercises sit at Learner's Book pp. 189–204), a citation slip in the guide that does not affect the teaching content; and the Grade 7 contents misnumbers the final Data handling unit as "Unit 21" although it follows Unit 30 — the body heads it Unit 31, and the placement records both numbers. The Grade 4 Unit 5 decimal comma (3,1 for three-and-a-tenth) is the book's consistent notation, recorded as printed, not treated as an error; decimals are written with the decimal comma throughout Grades 4–7. Record note N1: `phase3-push-state.json` still lists only 4 Zimbabwe folders (paperwork) although all 51 Zimbabwe files — including these 9 — are confirmed live on GitHub (sweep #8, 2026-11-05); the state-file gap is already on record, and this read re-confirms the 9 Maths files on GitHub at exact recorded sizes and hashes. Zimbabwe is **TEACHER-GUIDE EVIDENCE ONLY** in every lesson: every item is worded as what the guide states the teacher teaches and what learners should be able to do, Zimbabwe is named in the countries line and in the rows below, and it is added to NO lesson's acceptance count, on the Ghana precedent. Zimbabwe's class for Maths is **PARTIAL (evidence-only)**. Into this lesson the placements stock 7 teacher-evidence item(s).

## Zambia added 2026-11-11 (expansion merge)

The 2026-11-10 expansion read read Zambia's whole in-hand Maths set from the PDFs — **13 files, 626 PDF pages, and every one of them teacher evidence: the in-hand set holds no pupil textbook at all**. The expansion map is WRONG for Zambia Maths on both numbers: it listed 2 student + 7 teacher, but the true recorded split is 2 student + 11 teacher (the map's teacher count omitted the 4 KG Pre-Mathematics teacher files), and on opening BOTH files recorded as "student" proved to be teaching modules — the Grade 7 "Mathematics 2" file is the *Mathematics II Teaching Module, Form 1 Term 2* (its preface states it "is designed to help teachers prepare and deliver Competence-Based lessons"), and the KG1 "Pre-Mathematics and Science" file is the *Intellectual Disabilities Teaching Module, Term 3* (its cover is titled "TEACHING MODULE" and its interior is teacher guidance with teacher/learner role tables and a lesson-plan appendix). The two kind mis-records live in `textbooks.tsv` and are flagged in the expansion diff for the record-keepers — record-side, reported here, not fixed by this merge (merges never edit the filings or records). The set: ECE Pre-Mathematics and Science teaching modules (KG1 mainstream Terms 2 and 3, KG2 mainstream Term 3, and the Intellectual Disabilities adaptations for Terms 2 and 3); combined Mathematics and Science teaching modules for Grade 1 (Terms 2 and 3) and Grade 2 (Term 3) — only their mathematics content is placed here, the science halves are the placements' named exclusions; the Grade 5 *Numeracy Teacher's Guide* of the Catch Up Programme (MoE/VVOB, 2023), whose TaRL method — assessing each learner one-to-one and teaching learners grouped by their current level, not their grade — is Zambia's own adopted way of teaching foundational numeracy; and pure Mathematics teaching modules for Grade 4 (Term 3) and Grade 7 (Mathematics I Terms 2 and 3, Mathematics II Term 2). All 13 files were fetched fresh for the read (12 via the authenticated GitHub Contents API from the textbooks repo tree; the Grade 5 Catch Up guide via its recorded VVOB source URL at exactly the recorded byte size), verified by byte size, MD5, pdfinfo page count and cover/title page, and read in full; all 13 MD5s differ — no duplicates. Not in hand and cited nowhere: the CDC Digital Library *Mathematics and Science Teaching Module* for Form 2 (visual-impairment), which exists only as a banked lead with no file, and every Zambian pupil textbook, which sits behind a commercial wall (Longman/ZEPH/Oxford/MK) with no free file — the one further free lead is itself another teaching module, so it would not change the class even if secured. Zambia's own teaching ways are placed as content in their own right: local materials, Zambian languages, kwacha money and TaRL level grouping. Book errors found on the read are taught corrected and recorded in the placements: the Grade 7 Mathematics II module swaps the definitions of Profit and Loss in its Key Terms (printed p. 66), prints "the sum of exterior and exterior angles add up to 180°" where interior + exterior is meant (printed p. 57), mixes K600 000 with K60 000 and asks for "the price of the bag" in a laptop question in its end-of-topic assessment (printed pp. 77–78), gives a bacterium "6 300 000 meters long" (printed p. 9), and its cover and imprint-page ISBNs disagree; the Grade 7 Mathematics I Term 3 module's Pythagoras assignment sends a walker 3 km east and then 4 km **west** (printed p. 3) — as written no 3–4–5 triangle arises; the Grade 7 Mathematics I Term 2 module gives Lusaka–Kitwe as 845 km in one bullet and Kitwe–Lusaka as 357 km in another (printed pp. 15–16), prints "Complementary angles add up to 900" for 90° (printed p. 27), and carries a simplifying tip that reads as though unlike terms can be added or subtracted — only like terms combine (printed p. 6); the Grade 5 Catch Up guide leaves its own unfinished editorial query "(please check this line for meaning)" standing in the printed division lesson (printed p. 41) — the line it queries is still understandable and is placed as written; and the Grade 2 module's subtraction demonstration names the wrong character ("take 13 sticks from Samuel", PDF p. 37 — the module's characters are Bupe and Mutinta) and first asks for 13 sticks where its own word problem needs 15 (PDF p. 23). Zambia is **TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY** in every lesson: every item is worded as what the module or guide states the teacher teaches, Zambia is named in the countries line and in the rows below, and it is added to NO lesson's acceptance count, on the Ghana precedent. Into this lesson the placements stock 11 teacher-evidence item(s).

**Book 1:** *Numeracy Teacher's Guide, Catch Up Programme (Grades 3–5)* — Zambia, Ministry of Education with VVOB (compiled with Pratham, ZESSTA, TaRL Africa, UNICEF and others), 2023; 11,394,383 bytes, 52 PDF pp., MD5 4c1c78eb0f7fd45bab66bf5af99084c0. Teacher's guide — TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY; the TaRL method it scripts is Zambia's own adopted way of teaching foundational numeracy, and its activities are placed as content. Filed at Grade 5 in the library (the band's benchmark grade); the guide itself serves Grades 3–5. Fetched via its recorded VVOB source URL at exactly the recorded byte size. Printed page = PDF page − 3. Production note carried: the guide's own unfinished editorial query — "(please check this line for meaning)" — is left standing in the printed division lesson (printed p. 41); the line it queries is still understandable and is placed as written.

**Book 2:** *Mathematics and Science Teaching Module, Grade 2 Term 3* — Zambia, Ministry of Education, Curriculum Development Centre, Lusaka, 2026; 2,721,016 bytes, 96 PDF pp., MD5 df75641972d6ae099b1d6511b9a12899. Teaching module — TEACHER EVIDENCE ONLY. A combined module: only its mathematics sub-topic 2.5.1 is placed here; sub-topic 2.5.2 (Domestic Waste) is science and is excluded. Pages are PDF pages (the body's printed folios match the PDF pagination). Slips noted in the placements: one subtraction demonstration says Mutinta takes sticks "from Samuel" — a name carried over from another book's problem, the characters in this module are Bupe and Mutinta (PDF p. 37); and one addition demonstration first asks for 13 sticks where its own word problem needs 15 (PDF p. 23).
